\lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{13.61101pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\!\pushout\pushout
, translates to

\delta(n\lambda,\lambda n^{\prime}\!\!,\boxdot)=\delta(n,n^{\prime}\!\!,\boxdot)=~\pushout\pushout

which does not appear in
\delta
,
since, contrary to the
\mathsf{NDA}
,
the
\mathsf{2OTA}
does not operate on the boundary symbols.
However, this DAG rule yields an accepting state
if and only if
\pushout~=~\pullback
.
In that case, we can conclude that the starting state

\pushout\pushout~=~\pullback\pullback~=\boxdot\boxdot
.
Then,

\delta(n,n^{\prime}\!\!,\#)=\boxdot\boxdot

yields the accepting state
\boxdot\boxdot\in F
which is the starting state,
otherwise not.
A
\mathsf{2OTA}
accepts a picture of dimensions
(m,n)

if
q_{m,n}\in F
, which for
\Lambda
results in
q_{0,0}\in F
.
The state
q_{0,0}=q_{0}=\boxdot\boxdot\in F
iff
A
accepts
\Lambda
.
Consequently,
A
accepts
\Lambda
if and only
M
does, too.

For a nonempty input picture
P
, of dimensions
(m,n)
with
m,n>0
,
the rules
R
of
\mathsf{NDA}

A
applied to the picture itself, not the boundary,
correspond to the transition relation
\delta

of the
\mathsf{2OTA}
.
Let
\rho_{A}
denote a fixed run of the
\mathsf{NDA}

A
on
\cooDAG(\hat{P})

and
\rho_{M}
the corresponding run
of the
\mathsf{2OTA}

M
on
P
.
While
\rho_{A}
assigns two states
q_{1}
and
q_{2}

to the outgoing edges of any vertex
P_{i,j}
of a picture
according to the rules
R
,

\delta
assigns those states
as one state
q_{1}q_{2}
to the vertex
P_{i,j}
.
While
\delta
assigns only states to the picture itself,

\rho_{M}
additionally assigns the edge labels
q_{1}q_{2}\in Q

as one state to
the border
P(0,j)
and
P(i,0)
, identically to
\rho_{A}

which labels those outgoing edges with the states
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
.

For each accepting run
\rho_{A}
of the automaton
A
on
\cooDAG(\hat{P})
, we can construct an accepting run
\rho_{M}
on
\hat{P}
. The run
\rho_{A}
assigns two states
q_{1}
and
q_{2}

to the outgoing edges of each vertex
V_{i,j}
of
\cooDAG(\hat{P})
, where
i\in[m]_{0}
and
j\in[n]_{0}
, according to the rules from
R
. Then,
\delta
assigns those states as one state
q_{1}q_{2}
to the position
P(i,j)
:

1.

The string
\pushout\pushout
labels the outgoing edges of
all vertices
V_{i,0}
and
V_{0,j}
, where

i\in[m]_{0}
and
j\in[n]_{0}
. The automaton
M
assigns the same tuple
\pushout\pushout=q_{0}
to all positions
P(i,0)
and
P(0,j)
.
The corners
P(m+1,0)
,
P(0,n+1)
are
not
included.
They are irrelevant since their assignments are identical for every picture,
and the picture itself does not depend on them.
Unlike the
\mathsf{NDA}
,
the
\mathsf{2OTA}
does not even
reference the states assigned to these positions.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\forall i\in[m]_{0},j\in[n]_{0}:\rho_{A}(e_{i,0}^{\mapsdown}e_{i,0}^{\barrightarrowdiamond})&=&\rho_{A}(e_{0,j}^{\mapsdown}e_{0,j}^{\barrightarrowdiamond})=\ \pushout\pushout\ =q_{0}\\
&=&q_{i,0}=q_{0,j}=\rho_{M}(P(i,0))=\rho_{M}(P(0,j))\end{array}

2.

Now, using induction on
i
and
j
, we can show that if
\mathit{in}(V_{i,j})=e_{i,j-1}^{\barrightarrowdiamond}e_{i-1,j}^{\mapsdown}
and
\mathit{out}(V_{i,j})=e_{i,j}^{\mapsdown}e_{i,j}^{\barrightarrowdiamond}
, then
for
\alpha_{1}=\rho_{A}(e_{i,j-1}^{\barrightarrowdiamond}),\alpha_{2}=\rho_{A}(e_{i-1,j}^{\mapsdown}),\beta_{1}=\rho_{A}(e_{i,j}^{\mapsdown}),\beta_{2}=\rho_{A}(e_{i,j}^{\barrightarrowdiamond})
and
\sigma=P_{i,j}
, we have
(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\hbox to22.82pt{\vbox to5.2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 11.40979pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{}{}}}{{}{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{}{}}}{{}{}{}{}}}}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.98 L 1.19 2.98 C 2.83 2.98 4.16 1.65 4.16 0 C 4.16 -1.65 2.83 -2.98 1.19 -2.98 L -1.19 -2.98 C -2.83 -2.98 -4.16 -1.65 -4.16 0 C -4.16 1.65 -2.83 2.98 -1.19 2.98 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.85706pt}{-2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{{{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}}}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}{}}}{{}{}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-10.90979pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{10.90979pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{

{{{{{}}{}{}{}{{}{}{}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{

{{}}{}{
{}{}{}}
{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{{}{}{}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{{}{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.44 0 L 13.57 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.52975pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{10.00974pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\beta_{1}\beta_{2})\in R
,
\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\in\delta\left(q_{{}^{\mapsdown}}\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}q_{{}^{\barrightarrowdiamond}},\sigma\right)=\delta(q_{i,j-1},q_{i-1,j},P_{i,j})
and we can assign the state
q_{i,j}=\beta_{1}\beta_{2}
to position
P_{i,j}
.

3.

As the state assign to both outgoing edges of the vertex
V_{m,n}
must be
\pullback
, the state
q_{m,n}
must be
\pullback\pullback\ \in F
. Thus, we get an accepting run of
M
on
\hat{P}
.

Similarly, we can show that for each accepting run
\rho_{M}
of the automaton
M
on
\hat{P}
we can construct an accepting run
\rho_{A}
of the automaton
A
on
\cooDAG(\hat{P})
. This concludes the proof that (
1
) is true and the picture language
L
accepted by
\mathsf{NDA}

A
using encoding
\cooDAG
equals the language accepted by 2OTA
M
.

For the second direction,
let

M=(\Sigma,Q,\pushout,F,\delta)
be a
\mathsf{2OTA}
, where
\pushout\in Q
is the initial state of
M
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
M
assigns the initial state
\pushout
to the top and left border symbols of an extended picture, but not to any position with a symbol from
\Sigma
.
We can construct a
\mathsf{NDA}

A
simulating
M
as a triple

A=(N,\Sigma\cup\{\#\},R)
, in the following way:

1.

The
set of states

N=Q\cup\{\pullback\}
, also called
edge labels
, for a new state
\pullback\ \not\in Q
,
uses directly the
\mathsf{2OTA}
’s states
and requires additionally an edge label
\pullback
for acceptance
due to the lack of a set of designated final states.

2.

Contrary to an
\mathsf{2OTA}
whose transition relation
\delta

defines its run
\rho_{M}
only on the positions in an input picture
P
itself,
the rules
R
determine a run
\rho_{A}
on every edge of the DAG
\cooDAG(\hat{P})
.
Therefore, it uses the
alphabet

\Sigma\cup\{\#\}
,
including the boundary symbol
\#
.

3.

The
set of rules

R
reflects the starting state
\pushout

and accepting states
F

of the
\mathsf{2OTA}

as well as the transition relation
\delta

•

The rules for the position inside the picture itself, excluding the boundary, are constructed from
\delta

by propagating
\mathsf{2OTA}
’s computed state redundantly.

labels the outgoing edges

•

The
\mathsf{2OTA}

M
does not run on the boundaries below and right.
Consequently,
A
may assign arbitrary states to the edges pointing
from a picture vertex to such a boundary vertex.
By the following rules, we allow
A
to label those edges
just as edges not pointing to the boundary.
These rules continue on the right and bottom boundaries
to use the starting state
\pushout
of
M

for labeling edges pointing from boundary vertices to boundary vertices.

.

Evidently, the 2OTA
M
accepts the empty picture if and only if its initial state
\pushout
is final and the constructed
\mathsf{NDA}

A
accepts the DAG
\cooDAG(\hat{\Lambda})
.

For a nonempty input picture
P
, it is easy to verify that
equivalent runs for
M
on
\hat{P}
and
A
on
\cooDAG(\hat{P})
can be constructed with the rule set
R

in a similar way as for the first direction,
agreeing furthermore again on the acceptance of their inputs.

We have shown mutual simulation of the two models.
Thus,
\mathsf{2OTA}\eqcoo\mathsf{NDA}
.
∎

See
4.10

Proof.

The proof is by construction.
Consider the picture language
L\in\{\mathtt{a},\mathtt{b}\}^{*,*}

with a balanced number of positions labeled by
\mathtt{a}
and
\mathtt{b}
.
Formally:

L_{\mathrm{balance}}=\Bigl\{\>P\in\Sigma^{m,n}\Bigm|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|P_{i,j}|_{\mathtt{a}}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|P_{i,j}|_{\mathtt{b}}\text{ and }m,n\in\mathbb{N}\>\Bigl\}.

Neither a
\mathsf{2OTA}
nor a sgraffito automaton can recognize this language,
because both automaton models lack the ability to count.
But, a DAG automaton
A=(\,\{p\},(\Sigma,r),R)
, where
p
is a state and

R=(\lambda\hbox to22.36pt{\vbox to5.2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 11.1777pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.62497pt}{-2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\lambda)
, accepts a picture
P

if and only if
P\in L
.
Each edge labeled with the state
p
pairs exactly one
\mathtt{a}
with one
\mathtt{b}
.
The input-driven encoding yields
unconnected picture DAGs where every connected component
consists of two vertices, labeled
a
and
b
respectively, joined by an edge
labeled
p
.
∎
